#### Featured topics

![Image 39](https://www.oecd.org/adobe/dynamicmedia/deliver/dm-aid--ca6a40ff-75e7-4840-a67a-16c08e15b1d3/artificial-intelligence-circuit-board-brain-2024.jpg?preferwebp=true&quality=80)

[Artificial intelligence](https://www.oecd.org/en/topics/artificial-intelligence.html)

How to apply effective governance to harness the benefits of A.I. and mitigate its risks

![Image 40](https://www.oecd.org/adobe/dynamicmedia/deliver/dm-aid--264efc31-f759-4ff4-be72-584f3e9bc63b/shutterstock-2383728265.jpg?preferwebp=true&quality=80)

[Critical minerals](https://www.oecd.org/en/topics/sustainable-mining-for-development.html)

Critical minerals have become central to economic security as they are essential to energy and digital transitions, defence and advanced manufacturing

![Image 41](https://www.oecd.org/adobe/dynamicmedia/deliver/dm-aid--bc1603c8-ab2b-480e-8844-6f1a9f1eb117/future-of-work---shutterstock-2200894171.jpg?preferwebp=true&quality=80)

[Future of work](https://www.oecd.org/en/topics/future-of-work.html)

The world of work is changing rapidly as a result of new technologies, such as artificial intelligence (AI), the emergence of new forms of work, as well as greening, demographic change and globalisation

![Image 42](https://www.oecd.org/adobe/dynamicmedia/deliver/dm-aid--00799d67-6d1b-4c34-82ff-22b68ddc1416/l2---policy-issue---global-value-and-supply-chains---shutterstock-172537049.jpg?preferwebp=true&quality=80)

[Global value and supply chains](https://www.oecd.org/en/topics/global-value-and-supply-chains.html)

As the trend towards the international dispersion of certain value chain activities produces challenges, discover policies to meet these

![Image 43](https://www.oecd.org/adobe/dynamicmedia/deliver/dm-aid--8d3b6fa2-8657-4748-9636-6571d613597b/gettyimages-1282046119.jpg?preferwebp=true&quality=80)

[Investment](https://www.oecd.org/en/topics/investment.html)

The OECD's work on investment supports governments in attracting more and better-quality investment, prioritising security and sustainability

![Image 44](https://www.oecd.org/adobe/dynamicmedia/deliver/dm-aid--6160cca0-fad0-4ab9-8657-1c03be07ee22/pisa-2157565439.jpg?preferwebp=true&quality=80)

[Student performance (PISA)](https://www.oecd.org/en/topics/student-performance-pisa.html)

The Programme for International Student Assessment (PISA) assesses the skills of 15-year-old students in reading, mathematics and science as core domains

[Future of work](https://www.oecd.org/en/topics/future-of-work.html)

[Global value and supply chains](https://www.oecd.org/en/topics/global-value-and-supply-chains.html)

[Investment](https://www.oecd.org/en/topics/investment.html)

## Abstract

Korea’s fertility rate fell to 0.72 children per woman over her lifetime in 2023, the lowest in the world, while surveys show that people ideally would want more children. Employment and wage gaps between men and women are among the highest in the OECD, pointing to difficulties in combining careers and motherhood as a main culprit, combined with high spending on private education and housing. Family policies, labour market structures and gender norms combine to define the career-family trade-off, but high performance in one does not necessarily make up for gaps in the other two. OECD experience can guide Korea in its efforts to improve the situation. Korea has scaled up family policies considerably and compares well with other OECD countries on many indicators, but gaps remain to bring childcare fully in line with working parents’ needs. Parental leave eligibility is restricted and take-up low for a number of reasons including low replacement rates and weak legal protection against discrimination compared to OECD best practice. The adoption of flexible working arrangements is lower and working hours remain longer in Korea than in most other OECD countries, constraining the time available for family. Labour market duality leads young people to delay career starts and family formation and weakens their financial position. Social norms assign the responsibility for caregiving to mothers and charge fathers with being breadwinners much more strongly than in the rest of the OECD.
